## 2026-11-21 — Optimized-stack repeat Generate: support-only ZIP refusal and complete framework contract

**Verified user evidence:** the repeated Analyze/Generate on an already-optimized installation reported CET generic 0, semantic 0 and REDscript 0 new edits, but still produced a 15-file game-relative pass. The supplied ownership sidecar verified **14/15 files byte-identical to live installed source**, with only `G-GC/threshold.lua` changed (per-capture calibration); all 11 0-Engine files, the G-GC runtime and both CET native-location assets were identical. The screenshot's one CET "transform" is the source owner's `GC_CONTROLLER` transform, **not** another gameplay/source optimization. No REDscript source files were emitted. Three independent source refusals remain explicitly unproven: `late-system-read` (partial/drifted generated marker), `flushing-etiquette-input` (action order/receiver), and `pass8-chipware-throughput` (perk fallback). Do not silently classify these as complete without source proof.

**Fix in development:** GCO reads CET's authoritative `transforms[]`, excludes `GC_CONTROLLER` only from the **new optimization eligibility** count (retains owner accounting and G-GC calibration inside real passes). `CombinedOptimizationPassService` refuses support-only output **before ZIP staging/publication**. Page 2 ANALYZE uses isolated read-only CET and canonical REDscript generator previews to establish actual remaining source edits; GENERATE is disabled and console says `NO NEW OPTIMIZATIONS AVAILABLE` when both engines have no proven work. Hotscenes ENG-only remains an explicitly selected **Generate-only** operation, with current-source completed-marker recognition and no misleading `not applied` on an already English-only installation. Unproven source refusals remain diagnostically visible. One combined ZIP only.

**Framework user decision:** when at least one actual CET or REDscript optimization exists, every generated ZIP includes the **complete hash-verified G-REDruntime owner source inventory**. Source already present in the user's game is copied from those exact live bytes; missing files come from the verified bundled owner. New optimizer-stage output wins if a framework path was already staged. No framework, G-GC, 0-Engine, native-location or profiler-only ZIP when no substantive edits exist. Never overwrite original game source automatically. Tests cover support-only classification, verified full runtime set, installed-source precedence, manifest tamper and unmanifested file refusal. Note that the separate source-owner CET Resolver remains authoritative and unchanged.

## 2026-11-21 — Bundled G-REDruntime API visibility during REDscript Generate

**Root cause:** the full GCO app staged REDscript semantic optimizations before `CopyMissingFramework` added GCO's verified G-REDruntime sources to the generated ZIP. Owners #16, #29, #31–40 therefore inspected only existing `game/r6/scripts`, reporting ~19 API/ABI refusals even where the *same pass* would deliver the required InputHub, Scheduler and StateCache declarations. Custom Quickslots' #17 device-handler dependency is separate and may become satisfied once #16 applies; it must still be proven structurally.

**User-supplied archive examined:** `G-RedRuntime(1).zip` includes `Runtime.Get/IsReady/GetInputHub/GetScheduler/GetStateCache`, `InputHub.Subscribe/SubscribeDevice/Unsubscribe/UnsubscribeDevice`, `InputListener.OnGRedInput`, `InputDeviceListener.OnGRedInputDeviceChanged`, `InputEvent.actionName/actionType/consumed`, `Scheduler.Register/Unregister/RescheduleJob`, `ScheduledJob.Configure`, and `StateCache.GetPlayer/GetBlackboardSystem`. This is source evidence, not a claim that the user's previous ZIP was game-compiled.

**Implementation (development branch only):** `ProvidedRuntimeSourceScope` creates an invocation-scoped, read-only view of the validated `components/redruntime/owner` + original installed runtime, respecting `CopyMissingFramework`'s installed-file precedence. It verifies every manifest hash and source path; rejects missing/tampered/unmanifested bundle files; introduces no new runtime, copies and modifies no original source, and never changes capture/plan source hashing. `RedscriptGenerationPlan.Stage` optionally receives the packaged owner path from `CombinedOptimizationPassService`. Only the 8 ABI discovery readers plus VehicleMileage reschedule check use this effective view; generic rules, owner discovery and original source admission remain unchanged.

**Tests:** `test_phase2_provided_runtime.py` exercises no-bundle and supplied-bundle scheduler, input hub, state cache, conflicting installed source precedence, hash tamper and unmanifested source. The isolated CLI Stage accepts the signed manifest bundle path strictly for this replay. Expected full-pass behavior: eligible runtime-dependent semantic rules activate and `CopyMissingFramework` later includes exactly the files that were missing, respecting installed copies.

**Status:** [focused REDscript SCC #226 — SUCCESS](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/44933955825) at `1a5b17c30210e269ca956bcc505da3cb14f06e59`, including the six new bundled-runtime source-state cases and existing canonical source/SCC checks. Do not merge to `main` or claim 20/20 skips are eliminated merely from source inspection.

## 2026-11-21 — Checkpoint-driven CI for uninterrupted REDscript batches

- **Implementation commits no longer auto-run SCC or the full GCO package workflow on this development line.** Existing workflows retain `workflow_dispatch` for normal GitHub UI manual runs. Agents can create/fix/commit a complete pass without churn from auto-cancelled older SCC and full packaging runs.

Existing `redscript-phase2-checkpoint.yml` performs source-state, one-go composition, original source integrity and official SCC 0.5.31. Its concurrency group stays branch-specific (`redscript-phase2-${{ github.ref }}`), cancelling superseded focused checkpoints.
- **Full GCO milestone:** increment `.ci/checkpoints/full-gco.txt` only at integration/release checkpoints; `build-windows.yml` retains original .NET 10, sealed evidence, generator, ZIP/package and ownership checks, plus its optional manual replay inputs. Its concurrency is branch-specific (`gco-full-${{ github.ref }}`) with cancellation disabled.
- Changes to a marker trigger only its corresponding workflow, not the other. Normal commits, including workflow source edits, do not dispatch either workflow. GitHub Actions run history remains the authoritative result; a marker commit is a trigger, not a success claim.
- The change is confined to `dev/redscript/resolver_handoff`; if promoted into `main`, the integration/release agent must explicitly approve replacing main's prior per-push CI policy. Other agents' branches and stable releases are not modified.
- To start a run without the GitHub web UI: change the appropriate marker's `checkpoint=` value and push the commit. No extra GitHub connector action, API token or automation service required.

Markerless equivalent completed work, generic composition and exact idempotence are required.
- No semantic rule, C#/REDscript code, framework service or game ZIP was changed. This records acceptance criteria for forthcoming generator implementation. Still require actual source-state tests, full official CI SCC, gameplay feature validation and owner rollback/integrity checks.

Reopening a restored capture also leaves Analyze idle. `RefreshCompletedWorkspace` now re-verifies the restored index/hashes before enabling Analyze; changing capture never silently generates or applies source.
- **Normal summaries simplified:** CET: source count, measured callbacks, generic/semantic candidate counts, already optimized, **priority unresolved** vs **below-threshold unmatched callbacks** (e.g. 250 callbacks are NOT 250 missing mods). PresentMon status belongs in the independent top row. Page 2 footer no longer advertises player-side SCC.
- **Generation partial admission:** approved ten-rule `RedscriptGenerationPlan.Create` and `Stage` now catch **explicitly enumerated author-source-local refusal codes only** at rule-family boundary. A planning refusal is stored as `SOURCE_REFUSED` and reproducibly re-scanned against the exact unchanged installed source before staging; forged refusal fields fail. A staging refusal drops only that failed family's temporary output and continues remaining ordered rule families using the last safely composed state. `StageResult.Skipped` records rule order/id, eligible candidate count and exact source refusal. Unexpected errors, source/capture/plan drift, wrong outputs, generated postcondition failures and global integrity problems still refuse the entire pass. A source-local error currently defers the entire affected REDscript rule family, **not individual owners within that same family**; per-file partial emission would require source-owner generator changes and tests before claiming that granularity.
- **CET owner report carried through:** the bundled source-owner PassGenerator already composes safe work and emits categorized `skipOutcomes.entries` in its external companion manifest. GCO now reads that existing owner report, retains the numeric skip count, and writes categorized skip details plus REDscript skipped rule reasons into the `*.ownership.json` sidecar **outside** the clean game-relative optimization ZIP. After a successful Generate, UI lists count of not-applied CET entries, source-review subset and skipped REDscript families, and points to ownership sidecar. No replacement CET generator.
- **Added live behavior regressions:** one intentionally incomplete `STABLE_ARRAY_BOUND_V1` marker must become an independently verified `SOURCE_REFUSED` plan item while unrelated safe `PURE_FINAL_STATIC_BOOL_V1` files still generate; tampering a valid plan into a fake source refusal must fail before any output. ## 2026-11-20 — R5 stable ArraySize-bound source generator (verified)

**Owner instruction:** implement one additional genuinely broad source-only generic REDscript optimization, not a mod-specific semantic recipe. Leave `GenericBlackboardDefinitionPass` and both existing blackboard rules unchanged.

**Implemented on GCO `dev/redscript/resolver_handoff`:** `GenericStableArrayBoundPass.cs` owns `STABLE_ARRAY_BOUND_V1` as the tenth, final source-composed family in `RedscriptGenerationPlan`. The two CLI commands `scan-stable-array-bound` / `preview-stable-array-bound` use `SourceAdmission.ReadTokens`, the existing source hash/collection-provenance contract and the prior `effective-preview`. No GRSP instrumentation, native bridge, framework services, extra app UI or original installation writes. The canonical generator hoists a proven no-call, no-mutation `while i < ArraySize(localOrParamArray)` condition into an `Int32` local just before the loop, keeping exact body bytes, indexing and early returns. It rejects unproven method/array scope, array writes, nested loops, function calls, identifier collisions, drifted effective sources and incomplete generated markers. Generated markers carry the original loop-body SHA-256 to refuse edited partial output.

**Test contracts:** independent mod-owner source fixtures, out/parameter variables, previous-pass composition, untouched/partially/fully optimized states, generated-body tamper, source drift, no direct installed-source writes, canonical plan order and whole-stack source/GRSP/SCC independent parity. The real-source CI requires at least three **non-seven-semantic** owner folders with admitted current-source edits; the seven remain universally eligible to the generator. No measured speedup claims: `ArraySize` was not separately timed by GRSP.

**Verified:** [GCO CI #880 SUCCESS](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/44670023624), source `fa5844f0785a86273cbcc67e2e1e0208767544f6`. 196/196 managed tests, 7 player and 8 quest framework contracts; **2,319 private current sources**, **19 eligible stable-bound edits across 12 independent non-semantic owners**, 10 ordered generic rules/**173 total eligible edits**, **78 generated files**, independent manual-vs-ordered source parity, game-relative ZIP topology, six logged official REDscript SCC 0.5.31 phases (all exit 0), and integrated GCO .NET10 bundle complete. ## 2026-11-20 — source-local native service getter reuse (A), generator composition, A/B capture procedure

- **Implemented:** `LOCAL_SYSTEM_HANDLE_REUSE_V1` extends the existing `GenericLateSystemReadPass` and shared `SourceAdmission` token index, NOT a second generator/parser or mod-specific adapter. Recognizes two or more consecutive same-method and same-block `GameInstance.Get*System(localGame)` expressions whose service accessor is the leading unconditional expression of a plain assignment/declaration/direct call. Inserts one method-local inferred system handle; preserves all original downstream `GetFact/GetStatValue`, mutations, wrappers and callbacks. No cross-callback, cross-frame or global value cache. Refuses intervening braces, other services/receiver arguments, receiver reassignment, compound/short-circuited evaluation, comments inside the getter, and unrecognized partial markers.
- **Real installed source compiler baseline:** [GCO CI #804 SUCCESS](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/44636011168) with **181 managed tests**, existing late read + newly eligible local handle groups = **8 admitted edits in 6 files**, 10 total ordered generic rules/**160 eligible edits**/**67 staged files**, 2,319 original source files, seven official SCC 0.5.31 lanes, 323 GCO package contracts and bundled .NET10 app. Compared to #799, this added **7 source-local service reuse sites**. Not measured speedup.
- **Follow-up correctness/source composition:** generator now reads prior checked `effective-preview` bytes so another rule's source edits are not overwritten; it rejects any candidate count drift or invalid prior path. Canonical order remains unchanged and CI's independent manual-generation parity checks the composed same-file output. Added generated-state, partial/tampered-marker, conditionally evaluated getters, multi-owner and source-drift regressions. Latest hardened revision [CI #813](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/44637349153) was in progress when this record was written; do not claim its result without checking.
- **Measurement next:** use the sealed Nov 16 GRSP capture to rank actual getter work, but do not count its outgoing edges as savings. A valid paired post-install baseline/optimized game capture is required. [A/B capture and correctness contract](integration/REDSCRIPT-GENERIC-AB-CAPTURE-PLAN.md). No new gameplay capture, production REDscript ZIP or benchmark accepted yet.

Archive contains **zero** `coreclr.dll`, `hostfxr.dll`, `PresentationFramework.dll`, `vcruntime140.dll`, `msvcp140.dll` and no complete private .NET runtime tree.
- Source check at the pinned `v1.9.1_beta` tag confirms `DotNetRuntimeDetector.GetSystemWideDotNetBasePath()` uses `Environment.SpecialFolder.ProgramFiles` / `dotnet`; `AppDependencyChecker` additionally checks HKLM VC++ runtime registration. The app checks these **after process startup** and refuses startup when missing, regardless of a private `DOTNET_ROOT_X64` or merely app-local VC++ DLLs. In contrast, .NET framework-dependent apphosts can use private `DOTNET_ROOT_X64` for runtime resolution in general.
- **Result: zero system .NET/VC++ dependency with the unchanged official beta is NOT proven and is source-blocked by its own checker.** Do not pretend the current `app/` self-contained files form a reusable runtime root, or that including official portable ZIP alone eliminates dependencies.
- Plausible paths: obtain an upstream CapFrameX update that validates private .NET / app-local VC++ properly; alternatively build an explicitly modified CapFrameX from source and test runtime, signed native DLLs/overlay, licensing and .NET 10 deployment in isolated Windows without machine-wide dependencies. No CapFrameX binary modification, bypass, installation, release integration or end-to-end game test was performed.

## 2026-11-06 — PresentMon native MAX_PATH truncation found and fixed at GCO generator source

- User provided full RESULTS archive with CET and GRSP collected from the same world capture, a valid **43,732,842-byte** PresentMon frame CSV misnamed `PresentMon.cs`, and a `collection.json` with `FrameTimeCsv:null`. That is not evidence of PresentMon failing to capture. Its CSV includes `Application=Cyberpunk2077.exe` and `CPUStartDateTime` rows.
- Reconstructed the generated absolute target path from the user's original GCO package root and the capture session ID: `.../RESULTS/GCO-Capture-<32>/FrameTime/<32>/PresentMon.csv` is **exactly 260 characters**. The file written by PresentMon has 259 characters: `PresentMon.cs` (missing the final 'v').
- **Upstream authoritative proof:** PresentMon 2.6.0 `PresentMon/CsvOutput.cpp` function `GenerateFilename` uses `wchar_t path[MAX_PATH]`, `int pathLength = MAX_PATH`, and `_snwprintf_s(..., _TRUNCATE)`, then calls `_wfopen_s`. Filename extension truncation at 260 characters is consistent with the observed source and output, not a hook/hotkey/capture startup failure.
- Fix in **`PresentMonLifecyclePolicy.NewSessionCsv`**, the single GCO path generator: retain per-attempt uniqueness but use an eight-character attempt folder; guard the full absolute output path to at most 240 characters; for deeper installations fall back to `<session>/PresentMon.csv`, refusing overwrite; if even that cannot fit, fail optional frame-time capture with an actionable shorter-extraction-path warning before invoking PresentMon. `StartArguments` and the elevated controller also reject long start paths, while stopping an existing historical session remains possible. Move path generation inside the optional capture launch guard so CET/GRSP game launch still proceeds on a path-length refusal.
- Six direct contract tests cover reproduction of the exact 260-character old bug, safe new filename under a deep root, unique attempts, shorter fallback, CLI startup refusal, and failure if no path is safe. No fallback parser for `.cs`, no duplicate collector and no dependency on broken generated output. Existing recording remains user data and can be renamed manually to `.csv` for independent analysis, but GCO must not silently claim it was indexed. GCO stays v1.1.5, source owners unmodified; use only `build-windows.yml`. CI and real gameplay acceptance pending.

No additional Desktop runtime or external font installer. Palette retained: background #060D0F, card #081012, path #11191A, button #151C1C.

- The owner posted a native Windows screenshot of **v1.1.6** still showing **no outer yellow/cyan PNG border**, despite two prior CI-successful PNG presentation changes. Downloaded the exact [v1.1.6 package from CI #42364636686](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/42364636686) and inspected actual extracted PNG pixels. Both border assets exist at their intended `assets/ui/elements/` paths, are valid RGBA with sizes **1280×760** and **1080×640** and have bright yellow/cyan opaque painted pixels near the window perimeter. This is a **WPF image Source/measurement regression**, not an absent/blank/invalid PNG.
- Owner fix: bind `OuterBorderArtwork.Source` immediately when loading the two border bitmaps in `MainWindow.xaml.cs`, **before** checking `ActualWidth/ActualHeight`; a source-less WPF `Image` can have a zero desired size, leaving the old layout-dependent source initialization waiting indefinitely. Retain source-selection on later layout changes with non-null fallback. Log missing/undecodable border assets rather than silently losing the edge. Keep the full alpha PNG unmodified behind the controls, no WPF clipping/restored procedural border, current `v1.1.6`, 29-DIP inner frame and runtime pipeline unchanged.
- Added a safe `--verify-border-ui` STA branch to the existing GCO app: instantiate real `MainWindow` offscreen from actual delivered package, assert initial PNG image Source/dimensions **before layout**, layout & software-render actual WPF root to `RenderTargetBitmap` and check yellow/cyan image pixels visible near top border; exercise small-size PNG selection. Called by the **same canonical** `build-windows.yml` after packaging/manifest verification, before native launcher test. Previously passing CI without visual source verification is explicitly inadequate. New Windows CI must succeed independently; native Windows screenshot and 100/125/150% DPI still needed to confirm appearance.

## 2026-11-07 — Capture workflow: automatic owner-status refresh on Cyberpunk process exit

- Source review found a real UI workflow gap: `MainWindow` refreshed readiness at startup, on `Activated` and after buttons, but **not when Cyberpunk quit while the WPF window was already foreground/open**. This could leave `COLLECT RESULTS` disabled after a valid capture unless the owner clicked `REFRESH` manually. The native owner managers already report `captureReadyForCollection` / `CompletedCaptureCount`; the user should not have to restart or guess.
- Updated only WPF `MainWindow.xaml.cs` to use a **2-second lightweight GameRunning transition monitor**. It compares process-running state, performs owner status refresh **once per transition**, and defers that refresh while an install/collect/restore transaction is busy. The timer **does not poll the native managers every tick**, auto-starts on `Loaded`, stops on `Closed`, and still honors the existing source-owned capture/evidence/restore gates. The new transition refresh also handles secondary-monitor workflows where window activation events do not reliably fire.
- Keep existing `v1.1.6`, original owner PNGs, CET/GRSP/PresentMon, .NET10 runtime, one Windows workflow and all native restore boundaries. Validate canonical CI and independently test real game close → automatic Collect enablement; CI compilation alone cannot prove gameplay/capture success.

The lower corners have a short **3-DIP safety relief**, a minimum 17-DIP 45° chamfer and a 54-DIP run-out so the vector never draws over protruding yellow/cyan PNG art. No version bump or additional workflows.

## 2026-11-10 — Owner-approved 2.31 framework compatibility policy (decision only)

- Normal GCO profiling is **Cyberpunk 2077 2.31 only**, requiring exact **CET 1.37.1**, **RED4ext 1.30.0** and **redscript 0.5.31**. **0-Engine is the one open-ended version-range exception: >=0.18.0**, including newer releases without per-version approval; it is **not locked to 0.18.6**. Admission checks actual required injection interfaces and fails safely on structural incompatibility, not merely on an unrecognized future version number. Avoid unsupported early 0.17.x variants. 2.5 remains out of scope.
- Zero user CET/REDscript mods is valid. Frameworks are user-installed and never silently modified by GCO; show a precise actionable version mismatch/missing warning. GCO owns installation/restoration of its two profiler layers through their existing source-owner commands. REDruntime stays conditional; bundled PresentMon remains automatic and non-blocking; partial data still requires sound provenance and complete original-file restoration.
- This **later explicit owner decision overrides** the former independent CET-only/GRSP-only normal-admission requirement in older docs. Preserve correctness, missing-domain warning, session recovery and no-frame-only Continue safety. See `docs/product/CURRENT-DECISIONS.md` for the canonical support contract.
- **Documentation-only, no source changes**: framework version detection, game patch verification, mismatch refusal, actual install/capture and regression tests remain unimplemented. Last verified source/package CI is **#214 SUCCESS 185/185**, GCO v1.2.0; no new Windows workflow required for this record. Confirm live 2.31 installed framework identities before enabling admission.

- **Owner correction after initial documentation commit:** do not pin 0-Engine to the last current release; preserve the >=0.18.0 compatibility minimum, while other core framework versions remain exact. No source changes, and no CI was triggered by this documentation correction.

- **Latest owner clarification:** accept future 0-Engine versions by minimum-version rule without assuming each release needs a new GCO update. Actual integration still needs structural/injection contract checks; incompatible changes must produce a clear refusal/recovery path rather than silent modification of user mods. Documentation-only; no build or app behavior changed.

Preserve GCO v1.2.0 and the approved layout.

## 2026-11-10 — Implemented capture gates and corrected redscript compiler discovery

- **Implemented on GCO `main`, v1.2.0 retained:** core read-only Cyberpunk 2.31/framework preflight, two-owner CET/GRSP install status, attempt-identified Start/PresentMon without F11, Collect-click-owned PresentMon stop + native capture collection, console-first per-domain results, dual-native CONTINUE with optional frame CSV, safe owner restoration before Analyze, and separate RESTORE PROFILERS recovery. Capture proof records explicit collection attempt and verified GCO-owned PresentMon stop. Owner install/restore managers and original WPF design remain intact. Initial fully integrated [Windows CI #238](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/42848235343) **SUCCESS, 199/199 contracts**, artifact 13777779718, source `af90a1dd`. These are code/packaging tests, **not a verified in-game capture**.
- **Owner-discovered preflight defect:** `FrameworkPreflight.Inspect` wrongly treated redscript as `red4ext/plugins/redscript/redscript.dll`. The upstream 0.5.31 installer builds `engine/tools/scc.exe` and `engine/tools/scc_lib.dll` and packages `engine/config/base/scripts.ini` (see `jac3km4/redscript` tag `v0.5.31`, `resources/package.ps1`). redscript is **not** a RED4ext plugin. Source commits `1279e106` and `071c6991` now inspect the actual compiler paths. A file specifically named `engine/tools/scc.dll` is also recognized as **possible partial presence**, but is not automatically regarded as the required upstream `scc_lib.dll`. Missing, incomplete and version-unverifiable installations are distinguished; no paths are created, no game compiler is executed and no frameworks are altered.
- **Version-proof caveat:** read-only PE ProductVersion/FileVersion of `scc_lib.dll` or `scc.exe` is used when present. The official Rust build does **not necessarily provide usable PE version metadata**. If exact 0.5.31 cannot be proved, preflight **fails closed with 'redscript compatibility could not be verified'** rather than misleadingly claiming missing, guessing from file presence or silently admitting a wrong build. Follow-up must validate genuine 0.5.31 and 0.5.32 installed binaries and choose a trustworthy non-invasive version evidence source. Other framework version checks also require physical game-stack validation.
- **Latest completed source package:** [Windows CI #242](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/42853637516) **COMPLETED SUCCESS, 205/205 installation/CLI/package contracts** including physical redscript missing/partial/compiler-layout checks, owner CLI install/restore smoke, and packaged WPF both-page render. No native owner rebuild, additional CI workflow or version bump. **Live Cyberpunk 2.31 launch, capture and restore remain user validation gates.**

## 2026-11-11 — One-clean-capture-on-every-launch, explicit collection preserves everything (CI VERIFIED)

- User clarified: **COLLECT RESULTS never discards an attempt**. Once the user clicks it, GCO always stops its owned PresentMon, preserves/verifies whatever the three owners produced (including zero results), writes the current attempt index and reports exact missing/verified domains in the console. Collect remains possible after a game crash. It must never silently merge output from different launches.
- **Skipping Collect means abandonment** when the next Cyberpunk launch is observed while GCO is running, whether started by the GCO Start control or detected from a fresh external game process edge. For every new attempt GCO first requests a *verified graceful stop* of the previous GCO-owned ETW/PresentMon session, discards only GCO's known uncollected temporary folder, then creates a distinct current-session journal identity and a new PresentMon capture. The original installed native CET/GRSP profiler files and GCO recovery journal are retained. If stop/identity fails, refuse destructive cleanup; the console explains why.
- When GCO is **normally closed**, WPF cancels the close event until the same PresentMon stop completes and uncollected GCO output is cleared. If stop fails, the window stays open with an in-console retry diagnostic and restores the original Restore button usability. Collected evidence is never removed. An abrupt process crash cannot run this close callback; a subsequent recognized game launch reconciles the leftover owned PresentMon instead. New game launches with GCO previously closed or a game already running at UI startup are not misidentified as a verified process transition.
- GCO never deletes or rewrites live files in CET/GRSP owner game folders. **GRSP exposes no reset CLI**; GCO instead stores whether either native owner had completed results **before** the current attempt, and refuses to attribute those pre-existing results to a new session. This is conservative: it can skip later valid owner results until source-owner reset/provenance support exists. Its console now explains the exclusion. No native data from prior attempts can silently unlock Continue.
- **RESTORE PROFILERS remains available regardless of source preflight or PresentMon failure.** Manual Restore attempts to stop the owned ETW session and safely discard uncollected GCO outputs, then always invokes both authoritative native owner restoration managers even if the ETW stop failed. Source-owner restore proof still controls journal removal and path unlock.
- New folders use exactly `RESULTS/GCO-Capture-DDMMYYYY_HHMMSS/` with internal `collection.json` containing stable 32-hex session identity/game root; a same-second collision is explicitly refused (never overwritten or suffixed with a random hash). Siblings are `PresentMon/PresentMon.csv` and `PresentMon/PresentMon-controller.json`, `G-CET/`, and `G-REDscript/` only when actually collected. The random PresentMon subfolder is removed for new sessions. Old GUID and CI #257 date+GUID folders continue to resolve unchanged from their own index and paths. Native PresentMon's 240-character file-path limit is still enforced.
- `GCO-Incomplete-*` represents a read-only crash/recovery **diagnostic snapshot**, *not* a capture. Newly preserved snapshots now live under `RESULTS/Diagnostics/GCO-Incomplete-*`, with source-owned CET/GRSP logs intact and their SHA provenance in evidence.json. Already existing diagnostics and collected captures are not moved, overwritten or deleted.
- High-value contracts: prelaunch date/time and immutable ID, same-second collision refusal, flat PresentMon CSV and output integrity, both native owner folders and hash, legacy layout, collected preservation, verified-stop failure preserving pending evidence, safe uncollected deletion, unexpected/linked file refusal, owner-result admission and diagnostic isolation. ## 2026-11-11 — Event-driven game exit flush; uncollected CSV retained for manual Collect (CI VERIFIED)

- **Owner-approved final lifecycle:** when Cyberpunk closes or crashes, GCO automatically stops **only its own** PresentMon ETW session, waits for verified writer shutdown and CSV flush, and **retains** the current tracked CSV and uncollected capture index. It does **not** collect native evidence, delete the CSV, mark the attempt collected, or unlock Continue on game exit. The user's next **COLLECT RESULTS** preserves any available CET/GRSP/PresentMon evidence permanently (including partial/crash runs), and displays exact missing/verified sources; skip Collect and **Start again / Exit GCO / RESTORE** discards only safe uncollected GCO temporary data as previously approved. Collected evidence is never deleted by these actions.
- Replaced continuous 2-second game-process enumeration with native Windows **Win32_ProcessStartTrace** start notifications (System.Management event watcher) and per-game **Process.Exited** handle notifications. Both GCO-launched and externally observed game sessions are covered; one-time enumeration attaches to a game already running when GCO opens without misidentifying it as a new attempt. If Windows WMI start tracing is unavailable, a **10-second fallback timer** monitors starts; the exit handle remains event-driven. Events are serialized through the WPF dispatcher and defer while collection, launch or restoration owns the UI. The process PID and current journal session ID guard against a late exit from stopping a subsequent recording.
- One critical source change: `StopOwnedPresentMonAsync(sessionId, clearTracking:false)` is called only on game exit. It invokes the **existing elevated source-owned controller** but keeps `GCO-current-PresentMon.txt` pointing to the flushed CSV. Explicit Collect / abandoned new launch / normal GCO exit / Restore use the normal clearing path. A successful automatic stop can be reused during Collect or cleanup *without another elevation*, provided the same CSV is tracked and the owned process is verified absent. Failed shutdown is reported in the console and leaves all evidence for Collect to retry. No background polling, native DLL, profiler F11 handler or version change.
- Extended the *same* Windows CI workflow with an actual packaged OS-process event test: a short-lived Windows child is tracked through `Process.Exited`; when WMI is available, its WMI StartTrace event must be observed. No Cyberpunk, game file mutation, manual prompts or extra build workflow needed. GCO remains **v1.2.1**; gameplay crash/relaunch and WMI behavior on the user's Windows installation remain unverified until live testing.

## 2026-11-11 — Correction: exiting GCO preserves native capture state; only uncollected PresentMon is disposable (CI VERIFIED)

- **Latest owner correction supersedes CI #258/#259 normal-close semantics:** closing GCO must **NOT reset or abandon** the current profiler installation/session/capture. GCO immediately stops its own PresentMon on close, safely deletes **only the current uncollected GCO PresentMon.csv and its controller JSON**, and removes its tracking pointer. It never calls `AbandonCurrentUncollected` from window close. Collect is still the only action that permanently archives each available source, and a zero/missing native result never pretends to be valid. **Explicitly collected results remain immutable** (including an attempted Collect with a failed PresentMon stop). A **new game launch**, whether via GCO or an observed external start, safely discards the preceding uncollected GCO attempt and creates a fresh identity. **RESTORE PROFILERS** may also discard uncollected data when invoking the unchanged authoritative owner managers. GCO closing is *not* such a reset trigger.

On reopen with the same selected game root and no pending installation journal, it verifies the pointer and all archived evidence before restoring the Analyze page; different-game/stale/tampered pointers cannot grant a completed workspace. No new native semantics, crash snapshots, CaptureCache or version bump.

## 2026-11-12 — stop button artwork flicker on GCO focus revalidation (CI pending)

- Owner observed SVG stage buttons flash from Yellow/Restore to Unavailable and back whenever focus returns to GCO. Source proof: `Window_Activated` schedules `RefreshSourceAsync`; the refresh previously reset all four buttons' `IsEnabled` and both capture-state caches **before** awaiting `Workflow.InspectAsync`, despite still inspecting the same valid game root. `UpdateProfilerAction` also reapplied the same style and label on every refresh. This transient invalidation was a presentation regression, not a real admission decision.
- `GameInspector.SameVerifiedGameRoot` identifies an unchanged verified game root (case-insensitive Windows path). `RefreshSourceAsync` now retains the current button states and the previous inspection data during an **unchanged valid-root refresh**, applying changes only after fresh authoritative owner evidence is read. **Different or invalid roots still revoke all cached action permissions immediately**, and an actual failed package-provenance or owner-status inspection still disables unsafe actions. A durable journal always keeps **Restore** available, even if another inspection fails. This does not alter the underlying CET/GRSP owner permissions, capture admission, restore/rollback or the button SVGs.
- `UpdateProfilerAction` now updates its `Content` and `Style` only when the actual recovery state changes, avoiding another unnecessary template redraw. `SetBusy` intentionally continues disabling controls during real installs, launches, collections and restoration. Handlers retain their independent framework, owner, session and game-running admission checks.
- Added regression contracts for same-root and case-only refreshes versus different, invalid and missing roots. Product version held **v1.2.3**. **Canonical Windows CI #277 VERIFIED SUCCESS:** [run](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/43145885305) at exact source `e1521be53199fdb90576be7f194a28433f2bb432`. **278/278** installation/CLI/package contracts PASS, packaged WPF assets/cards/console clearance and both pages rendered at all tested sizes PASS, CET+GRSP .NET 10 CLI and source-owned GRSP install/restore PASS, final GCO **v1.2.3** manifest and ZIP PASS. ## 2026-11-13 — GCO-only durable capture lifecycle and native tray (v1.2.5; gameplay pending)

- **Explicit owner decision:** standalone CET and GRSP repositories, stable releases and independent manager behavior remain unchanged. GCO alone controls when captures are retained/abandoned. The *disposable, exact-pinned vendor checkouts in GCO CI* receive source-level integration adaptations through \`integrations/three-profiler-test/Apply-GcoCaptureLifecycle.ps1\`. No shipped-release binary patch, no changes to either standalone repository and no second .NET runtime.
- **Bundled GRSP native variant:** source-built Rust DLL from the pinned GRSP checkout + verified official release Cargo.lock. RED4ext shutdown no longer implicitly completes active capture. A successful explicit F11 STOP/export writes \`GCO-F11-Complete.flag\`; the bundled GRSP manager accepts completed captures only with summary, status and exact flag. A crash before F11 STOP is not collectable. Completed F11 output survives later game exit/crash. Standalone GRSP retains its historical implicit shutdown export.
- **Owner-scoped reset:** both bundled managers expose \`--discard-live\`, gated by managed ownership, closed game and no reparse links. CET retains original native F11 and its standalone \`--reset\`; GCO's bundled \`--restore\` and GRSP's bundled \`--restore\` discard live native output without creating permanent component archives. GCO refuses a new game launch until **both** owner resets and owner status reinspection succeed; partial reset failure preserves the journal for recovery.
- **GCO-owned collection:** each bundled owner receives a session-bound temporary \`GCO_COMPONENT_RESULTS_ROOT\` under \`GCO/RESULTS/.staging/<session>/<owner>\` for \`--collect\` only. GCO copies and hashes owner archives into its indexed \`GCO/RESULTS/GCO-Capture-...\` folder, then retires verified temporary owner archives. Only a successful explicit Collect with at least one verified native F11 capture seals the session. PresentMon alone never qualifies as complete native evidence. COLLECT clears remaining CET/GRSP scratch; Capture Again discards uncollected GCO output including indexed PresentMon or interrupted/unsealed owner copies, preserving collected archives, manual/foreign files and integrity checks.
- **GUI unload/tray:** the existing native Win32 root launcher retains a small single-instance tray host without CLR after WPF closes. Clicking its icon rebuilds the WPF interface, which resumes its durable game root, session and Collect/Restart/Restore state. Closing is no longer a discard or PresentMon cleanup trigger. While GUI is unloaded, a low-frequency native game-process check requests graceful GCO-owned PresentMon stop after game exit; tracked CSV remains until Collect/Start Again/Restore. Reopening re-arms the watcher for subsequent attempts. Elevated stop may require UAC; a canceled/failed stop preserves evidence and must be retried after reopening. The tray can be exited safely only when no PresentMon process remains while a tracked capture exists.
- **Restore:** owner-verified native restore clears the active journal and the old Analyze navigation bookmark without deleting already collected archives; ordinary CAPTURE/INSTALL PROFILERS view resumes. CONTINUE after a valid completed collection still restores native files and opens Analyze. The approved WPF 1280×760 Normal, smaller layout, fullscreen artwork and two-page design are unchanged; GCO product version remains **1.2.5**.
- **Verified partial CI:** initial native tray link failed in #316 (missing shell32 linker import), repaired at source. [Windows CI #317](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/43516281824) **SUCCESS** with native tray launcher + integrated manager/native build. [Windows CI #318](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/43516665150) **SUCCESS**, 282 core/package contracts, real bundled GRSP CLI explicit-F11 marker admission, crash-output refusal, owner-scoped discard, non-archiving restore and one private .NET 10 runtime. Later source changes extend unsealed-copy, reopening and tray re-arming contracts; final integrated run **pending** until checked. These are runner/fixture tests, not user gameplay proof.
- **Still unverified in real game:** CET+GRSP synchronized F11 completion, interrupted F11/game crash, GCO closed during game and native tray ETW flush/UAC, multiple sequential captures, exact physical RESULTS content, memory footprint of native host, alternate DPI and restoration on the user's installation. Do not declare feature shipped/gameplay-accepted on CI alone.

### 2026-11-13 — explicit Restore/Recapture must be full capture reset (Windows CI pending)

- Owner re-confirmed the only user-visible contract: **Collect** saves the verified current attempt to root `RESULTS`; **START CYBERPUNK again** abandons any *uncollected* attempt (no F11/export needed), resets both bundled owner-managed live scopes and starts a fresh attempt; **RESTORE PROFILERS** abandons the *uncollected* attempt, verifies original files and returns to INSTALL. Previously explicitly collected captures remain immutable.

On partial failure the recovery journal survives, allowing the same RESTORE button to finish cleanup. Final journal deletion happens only after both owner state verification and GCO scratch reset. WPF calls that single transaction, rather than separately catching/suppressing discard failures.

### 2026-11-13 — explicit buttons: no implicit collection prerequisites for discard

- **GCO-only scope:** user reapproved three actions: Collect archives the current successful F11 results; START again forfeits uncollected data and begins a new session; RESTORE forfeits uncollected data and returns to Install with original profiler files. Previously explicitly collected archives are permanent. Neither Restore nor Capture Again requires a prior Collect or completed F11. Both do require the game stopped, source-owned reset/restore verification and safe PresentMon shutdown.
- Fixes `2fc16bbb`–`aff1ef92`: native owner restoration may still proceed if PresentMon shutdown fails, but final journal deletion and INSTALL-ready success now require the owned writer stopped and GCO's uncollected session discarded. On partial failure the journal remains; another RESTORE call safely completes remaining cleanup. No snapshot/archive is created just because Restore was clicked. The same core workflow owns cleanup; the WPF restore button no longer suppresses a discard failure and then claims clean completion.
- Regression `a529b9c9` covers a GCO pending session containing PresentMon scratch but **no completed F11 or prior collection**: explicit abandon removes the owned temporary capture and allows a different fresh session ID, retaining the installed-session journal until deliberate restore.
- `278c010c` removes a non-elevated `Process.MainModule`/executable-path precheck from PresentMon stop handling. When a GCO CSV pointer exists, the UI only checks the process name to avoid an unnecessary privilege failure; the existing **elevated owner controller** remains the single authority to verify executable identity and stop the named ETW writer. When no GCO pointer exists, unrelated PresentMon is not touched.

### 2026-11-13 — short UI results / owner-independent Restore / one PresentMon UAC per capture

- **Exact owner scope:** only GCO UI lifecycle, result-handling and the existing bundled PresentMon CLI argument were changed. No standalone CET/GRSP repository changes, CapFrameX work, new helper/service, graphics/layout modifications, new workflow or version bump. Product remains **v1.2.5**.
- **PresentMon automatic stop:** tagged official [PresentMon v2.6.0 OutputThread.cpp](https://github.com/GameTechDev/PresentMon/blob/v2.6.0/PresentMon/OutputThread.cpp) tracks the named target on process start and invokes `ExitMainThread()` when the last target exits if `--terminate_on_proc_exit` is set. The existing GCO elevated start CLI now passes that flag; one capture-start UAC launches its writer. Collect/Restore/game exit no longer shell-elevate a second `--presentmon-control stop`. The native tray does not launch an elevated stop worker or timer. GCO awaits the writer PID recorded in the elevated startup report using nonprivileged Windows process enumeration; a timeout preserves tracking and gives a short retry status. Historical explicit owner stop CLI still exists for recovery/diagnostic compatibility but is not in the ordinary UI/tray lifecycle.
- **Restore:** `UnifiedProfilerWorkflow.RestoreOwnedAsync` attempts BOTH source-owner manager restores independently even if the initial status parse fails or either manager errors. It verifies the final clean game state from both owners. If one restore fails and originals are not verified, the durable journal remains for retry and the UI reports a short Restore incomplete status. If both owners prove clean despite one manager's post-operation warning, the verified original state takes precedence. PresentMon stop/temporary result cleanup is a separate finalization gate, never a reason to skip restoration of CET/GRSP originals.
- **UI:** compact CET/REDscript/PresentMon saved/missing summary and next action on Collect; concise game-exit and Restore statuses; full error details in Trace only, not hover or scrolling essays. Existing capture workflow/card geometry unchanged.
- **Contracts:** Existing Core test now expects `--terminate_on_proc_exit` and still rejects any PresentMon F11 hotkey; packaged Windows `--verify-game-process-events` additionally asserts its managed stop entrypoint **cannot invoke a second elevation**. The exact latest source at `5dd724456700` is in canonical [Windows CI #350](https://github.com/giorgioherma/Gs-Cyberpunk2077_Optimizations/actions/runs/43525354643), **pending when written**. Earlier #344 succeeded but does not cover these changes. Real Cyberpunk crash/exit and UAC behavior are still manual acceptance tests.
